In honor of Jackie the bald eagle, Ojai Raptor Center fills room with cards, drawings, and tributes

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Published Nov 10, 2026 1:15 PM EST

A “room filled with kindness” is opening at the Ojai Raptor Center in southern California. After the center’s staff cared for the beloved, internet-famous bald eagle (Haliaeetus leucocephalus) mother Jackie before her death in August, they received thousands of letters, drawings, cards, and well-wishes from people all over the world. Now, they have taken them all and turned a room into a place where the staff can reflect and read these messages of support.

“We wanted to share this special space with you, because you helped create it,” the center writes. “From all of us at Ojai Raptor Center and our extended care team, thank you for your compassion, kindness, and support. Patient 26-519 (Jackie’s) journey brought people together from around the world through a shared love and concern for wildlife. We hope that same compassion continues to support wildlife rehabilitation centers everywhere.”

The team at Friends of Big Bear Valley (FOBBV), who run the 24/7 livestream of the nest that Jackie shared with her partner Shadow, thanked the center’s staff, medical team, volunteers, and all who sent in cards.

“Your kindness and caring means more than words can express,” FOBBV writes. “We have the best eagle family and we are so grateful for you all.”

Jackie the bald eagle was born, raised, and lived in Big Bear Valley in Southern California. Prior to the 2011-2012 nesting season, no bald eagle chicks were known to have ever hatched in Big Bear Valley. Then came Jackie, named after a forest ranger, and born to eagle parents Ricky and Lucy in 2012.

During the 2016-2017 nesting season, Jackie took over a nest where Friends of Big Bear Valley (FOBBV) had a camera stationed. In 2018, she had two chicks named Stormy and Big Bear Baby (or BBB) with her first mate, Mr. B.

Her long-time partner Shadow entered the picture in summer 2018. He showed up at the nest and would not leave. FOBBV called the new male Shadow because he appeared so determined to stay in the nest, and the team initially believed he was one of Jackie’s siblings, also named Shadow. The eagle’s plumage indicated he was one year older than the other Shadow and not Jackie’s sibling, but the name stuck.

Shadow continued to hold his ground and Mr. B gave up and eventually left. Jackie then accepted Shadow as her new mate.

In 2025, Jackie laid three eggs that all hatched in early March. However, a strong snowstorm dumped up to two feet of snow and battered the nest with strong wind and only two of the chicks (Sunny and Gizmo) survived.

In January, Jackie laid two eggs in the nest, but ravens destroyed them. She later laid two more eggs that hatched in April and named Luna and Sandy. The name Sandy is in honor of FOBBV’s former executive director Sandy Steers who died from cancer in February.

On July 17, Jackie was found injured on the ground near Dana Point Park, California. Cell phone video from a witness confirmed that she had an altercation with two other subadult eagles. She successfully fended off the other eagles by turning over and using her talons, but could not fly far away.

She was transferred to the Ojai Raptor Center, where she was diagnosed with severe anemia. Her anemia worsened during the first week of August, the Ojai Raptor Center said that she was in unstable condition on August 9.

Jackie died early in the morning on August 10. Jackie’s necropsy revealed what the Ojai Raptor Center called a “complex and unusual disease process involving severe, chronic inflammation and ulceration of the stomach, persistent internal bleeding, anemia and secondary bacterial sepsis.” She was also diagnosed with Highly Pathogenic Avian Influenza (HPAI) commonly called bird flu, which was the cause of the severe stomach ulceration. Raptors (eagles, hawks, falcons, owls, etc.) are highly susceptible to the virus.

A memorial service was held on September 5 in Fawnskin, California, along with a second celebration of life called Wings Over Big Bear on October 3. The event featured representatives from FOBBV, the Yuhaaviatam of San Manuel Nation, and more.

Jackie’s resilience inspired millions of people around the world. To honor her memory, FOBBV accepts donations to support its conservation efforts in the area. Supporters can also donate to Ojai Raptor Center’s Bald Eagle Recovery Campaign.

He Wants The Job: Conte is Man Utd's Only Better Option Than Pochettino

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Manchester United are expected to sack manager Michael Carrick this week, according to GIVEMESPORT sources.

Following internal talks on Monday, Man United chiefs Jason Wilcox and Omar Berrada are expected to recommend the dismissal of the 45-year-old to Sir Jim Ratcliffe in a meeting held in Monaco, GMS understands.

The Red Devils have produced two remarkable comebacks in the last week, coming from 2-0 down to beat AS Roma in the Champions League, and twice coming from two goals down to draw 4-4 with Aston Villa in the Premier League at Old Trafford on Sunday.

However, the point against Unai Emery's men wasn't enough to take United out of the relegation zone, which shows how poor their season has been.

With 10 games played, they have registered just the one victory at home to newly-promoted Ipswich Town, and they could be bottom of the table by the time they kick off against Liverpool in their next match, should Crystal Palace beat Coventry City and Sunderland record at least a draw against Villa.

Wilcox and Berrada have clearly seen enough, having given Carrick a reprieve following the heavy defeats against Como and Bournemouth, and now it will be up to Ratcliffe to make the final decision.

With the expectation that he will leave, GMS revealed that United States manager Mauricio Pochettino is now a leading contender for the job if it becomes available.

The Argentine has barely been mentioned among the talk of possible Carrick replacements in recent weeks, but he has now emerged as a strong candidate, with $10m (around £7.5m) needed to activate the release clause in the contract he only signed in August.

The former Tottenham boss isn't the only manager admired, with appreciation at board level for two out-of-work managers in Eddie Howe, who they have held talks with according to Fabrizio Romano, and Julian Nagelsmann too.

Another manager currently available is Antonio Conte, and he is the only frontrunner for the job who would be a better appointment than Pochettino.

The one big concern with Conte is whether he has the patience to first build, and then stick around for, a project at a club, given what we have seen in the past.

However, if United are looking for a manager to make an immediate impact and who is a proven winner, then the Italian is the obvious choice, and he wants the job.

There is no doubt that the 57-year-old would send a much-needed rocket up some Red Devils stars who are performing nowhere near the levels they were in the second half of last season.

He should instantly gain the respect of the squad too, given he has won league titles at Chelsea, Juventus, Inter Milan and Napoli.

Conte won those trophies in his first seasons in charge of Chelsea, Juventus and Napoli, proving that he knows how to be successful quickly.

Napoli had finished 10th the season before they won the Scudetto under the Italian manager, so he has shown that a big improvement is possible, as would be needed at Old Trafford if he took the reins now.

Conte can be volatile, but he has the passion and tactical nous to get results and change a team's fortunes, and he is the outstanding choice on the market for many reasons.

Pochettino has clearly shown he is a manager with a big personality who has held down huge jobs, but he doesn't have the proven success of winning trophies like Conte does.

Vincent Kompany has returned to Bayern Munich’s training ground for the first time since his collapse.

The 40-year-old fell ill during a reserve-team training session on Friday 23 October and was taken to hospital, where he was diagnosed with meningeal irritation.

He had been unwell with a viral infection earlier that week, which forced him to miss the pre-match press conference ahead of Bayern’s visit of Arsenal in the Champions League. He was able to be on the touchline for that match, which took place two days before his collapse, but he was nevertheless subdued in the dugout.

The Bundesliga champions’ director of sport Max Eberl said his return would take “weeks, not days” as Bayern insisted he would only return when he was back to full health.

And on Tuesday morning, Kompany was spotted at Bayern’s Sabener Strasse training complex, with German newspaper Bild circulating photos of the Belgian in the club’s offices.

He appeared to have his left arm in a sling, which is said to be due to a dislocated shoulder suffered during the fall.

The club have not yet put out a statement confirming his return. The Independent has contacted Bayern Munich for comment.

Kompany could now be in contention to make his comeback in the dugout when Bayern host Koln on Friday 20 November after the international break.

The club have won four and drawn one since assistant manager Aaron Danks took the reins from Kompany, only dropping points in a dramatic 2-2 draw with Borussia Dortmund in Der Klassiker.

Bayern are top of the Bundesliga table with 21 points in nine games, level on points with Dortmund but ahead on goal difference.

During Kompany’s time away, Bayern talisman and England captain Harry Kane has also brought the Ballon d’Or back to Bavaria, pipping Lamine Yamal and Kylian Mbappe to football’s top individual prize.

Kane paid tribute to Kompany in his acceptance speech, who was still in hospital at the time.

He said: “To Vinnie, I know he's at home or I think still in the hospital so wishing you very well. Hope this has gave you a bit of energy.

“To Vinnie and all the staff, the way we train, the way you push us, it's a credit to yourself. Since I've been with you, I've improved as a player, no doubt, improved as a person, as a leader, and that's thanks to your leadership.”

Pete Hegseth decries impeachment push, defends Iran war handling

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Secretary of Defense Pete Hegseth on Tuesday railed against the Republican congressman who introduced articles of impeachment against him and aggressively defended his leadership in the Iran war and other military operations.

"What one disgruntled and defeated congressman is attempting is not oversight, and it's not accountability. It's a political spectacle, a personal manifesto driven by ego and resentment," Hegseth said a day after Rep. Thomas Massie, R-Ky., put forward the eight impeachment articles.

"It's a farce, and it's a stunt, and everybody knows it," Hegseth said during his first Pentagon news conference in six months.

The articles accuse Hegseth of waging war in contravention of Congress, carrying out extrajudicial killings tied to boat strikes in the Caribbean against purported drug smugglers, and suppressing free speech by targeting Sen. Mark Kelly, D-Ariz., a veteran.

Massie, a libertarian who lost his Republican primary after clashing with President Donald Trump, kicked off the impeachment battle less than a week after a blue-wave midterm that will flip House and Senate control to Democrats.

Rep. Hakeem Jeffries, D-N.Y., who is poised to be speaker of the House in the next Congress, on Sunday said Democrats are a "hard yes" on impeaching Hegseth.

The House is set to vote Tuesday on a procedural motion related to the impeachment resolution. If it passes, the measure will proceed to a debate and vote. Impeachment resolutions are privileged, meaning the House must act on them, table them or vote to refer them to committee within two legislative days.

Hegseth accused Massie of performing a "stunt" that sends dangerous signals to America's enemies.

"It puts a target on the backs of American warriors by signaling that Congress will undermine their missions in real time," Hegseth said. "This is not patriotism. This is sabotage."

Trump piled on, writing in a Truth Social post right after Hegseth's presser ended that the Pentagon chief "gets an 'A' PLUS, PLUS, PLUS for his defense against a disgruntled, angry, defeated, former Congressman, Thomas Massie, a TOTAL LOSER!"

Trump, who was impeached twice during his first presidential term, said Monday that he would stand by Hegseth even if the House impeaches him.

Republicans still hold a slim 218-214 majority in the House, but it is unclear if the rest of the GOP conference will be able to band together to crush Massie's impeachment resolution. Some Republicans, notably including Vice President JD Vance, have directly blamed their midterm losses on the Iran war, which continues to see intermittent fighting amid an uneasy stalemate.

If Hegseth is impeached, the Senate will hold a trial and vote on whether to remove him from office. A two-thirds majority is required for conviction and removal. No U.S. Cabinet secretary has ever been removed by the Senate.

— CNBC's Garrett Downs contributed reporting.

The latest Zelda game is "off to a great start," says Nintendo president Shuntaro Furukawa

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Nintendo says The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time remake is "off to a great start" and the 40th Anniversary Edition Switch 2 console has also been "very positive," heading off speculation from an investor that price hikes may be hurting momentum.

Taking questions as part of its latest financial briefing, Nintendo president Shuntaro Furukawa is pressed on how well the Switch 2 is selling its wares after the console's price jumped to $500 earlier this year. Investors reckon things are slowing down, and they don't like that. As such, one asks the Nintendo suits whether they think current financial goals will still be met.

The short answer is absolutely; just look at how well Zelda's Ocarina of Time remake and 40th-anniversary console are doing.

"Adoption has progressed smoothly over the roughly one and a half years since Nintendo Switch 2 launched last June, and the installed base now exceeds 25 million units," Furukawa says. "At the end of last year's holiday season, we explained that there were regional differences in how well our expectations were met. But the March release of Pokemon Pokopia acted as a driver of global hardware sales, and we entered the new fiscal year with good momentum."

Furukawa continues by saying that, while the Switch 2 did indeed increase in price, the impact hasn't been too great because most people were given plenty of notice and have had plenty of hardware and software bundles to compensate for it.

That takes us to today, where Nintendo is seemingly riding high thanks to all things Zelda. Furukawa says the special version of the Switch 2 designed to celebrate Zelda's 40th anniversary has been "very positive" for the company and has "contributed to strong sales in various regions." Meanwhile, while it's too early to delve too far into the Zelda: Ocarina of Time remake numbers, Furukawa says it's "off to a great start," and that Nintendo is in "good shape for the coming holiday season," too.

"Holiday season sales significantly impact the number of units shipped annually for our dedicated video game platform business," he says. "Given the hardware price revisions, we think the sales trends over the next several weeks will be key to achieving our sales guidance."

While no official numbers are given, there are signs that, yeah, those Zelda products are shifting units. As per Circana, the Zelda 40th Anniversary Switch 2 drove record sales for Nintendo in the US. In fact, it was only last month that a Nintendo exec said those at the house of Mario "underestimated" how well it would sell. Stock has been an issue, though a welcome one.

We have less to go on with the Zelda: Ocarina of Time remake. That said, analyst firms say the N64 throwback is the largest physical launch in the UK so far this year. With the festive period coming up, I can see why Nintendo isn't sweating this one too hard right now.

Why Marvel Fans (And Me) Think Tom Holland’s Doomsday Appearance Just Got More Likely

Robert Downey Jr. Coming Back For Doomsday Destroyed One Marvel Record (And Paul Bettany Finally Got Asked About It)

I’ve Been Wanting Kit Harington To Return To The MCU, And Someone Finally Asked Him About It

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

Nebula is one of my favorite MCU character.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The Marvel Cinematic Universe is nearing its 20-year anniversary, and many fans have been watching the Marvel movies in order since Day 1. The shared universe (which is streaming with a Disney+ subscription) has a few fan favorite franchises, chief among them being James Gunn's Guardians of the Galaxy. I've been missing Nebula and the rest of the team, but Karen Gillan recently gave me some hope about their return.

The end of Guardians of the Galaxy Vol. 3 saw the team splitting up and going their separate ways. The final title card confirmed that Chris Pratt's Star-Lord would return to the MCU eventually, which put doubt into whether or not we'd ever see everyone else. In an interview with The Direct, Gillan was asked if there's a chance we'll see Nebula back in upcoming Marvel movies, with the actress responding with:

Maybe... You know I can't tell you that [laughs]. I have Marvel snipers on me. I have definitely spoken to them. Whether or not that was about making another appearance or not is a different story. Okay, waffle without saying anything at all. I'm still going. Basically, no spoilers over here.

Honestly, I'll take it. This isn't a confirmation about if/when we'll see the Blue Meanie back in the shared universe, but the fact that Karen Gillan is in contact with the powers that be at Marvel is a hopeful message. Nebula is one of my favorite characters in the entire MCU, and I'd love any opportunity to catch up with her sometime in the future.

When we last saw Nebula, she and Drax were committed to staying on Knowhere and raising the children that they saved from the villainous High Evolutionary. I'd love to se how this was going, as well as how Karen Gillan's character stands with her the revived Gamora. There are certainly narrative threads to pull from, so let's hope that the studio moves forward with a project that includes Nebula.

James Gunn is departing as co-CEO of DC Studios, which might offer some hope for Guardians of the Galaxy fans who want to see more from the cosmic team of heroes. Now that his schedule is freed up, perhaps he'll be inspired enough to explore what happened after Vol. 3. After all, Marvel fans adore the Guardians.

For now fans can re-watch Karen Gillan's tenure as Nebula on Disney+. That includes the three Guardians movies, the Holiday Special, as well as appearances in Avengers: Infinity War, Endgame, and Thor: Love and Thunder.

The next MCU movie hitting theaters is Avengers: Doomsday on December 18th as part of the 2026 movie release list. We'll just have to see if the Guardians of the Galaxy end up popping up in that movie or its sequel Secret Wars.

Man Utd, Arsenal, Chelsea-linked wonderkid drops January transfer hint

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

By Oliver Thomas | 10 Nov 2026 13:12, Last updated: 10 Nov 2026 13:13

Swedish wonderkid Oliver Mansson has hinted that he could leave IFK Goteborg in the January transfer window amid reported interest from some of Europe’s biggest clubs.

Widely regarded as one of Scandinavia's brightest teenage prospects, Mansson has now established himself as a first-team regular with Goteborg at the age of just 17.

The deep-lying midfielder has started each of the last 14 Swedish Allsvenskan matches and his performances are understood to have caught the attention of a number of Premier League sides.

Champions Arsenal and London rivals Chelsea were both credited with an interest in Mansson last month, while Manchester United are also understood to have entered the race.

Scouts from Italian club Roma, German outfit Freiburg and EFL duo Watford and Leicester City were allegedly present for Goteborg's 1-1 draw against Djurgarden last month where both Chelsea and Man United also had representatives keeping tabs on the midfielder.

All of these clubs seemingly face stern competition from La Liga champions Barcelona, though, after Mansson revealed that he “was rooting for Barca” when he was younger.

Nevertheless, Barca’s interest is not thought to be as strong as other suitors, and all options remain “open” for Mansson, who is refusing to rule out making the next step in his career at the start of next year.

Asked when he will make a decision over his future at Goteborg, Mansson told Swedish news outlet Sportbladet: “You talk to your agents about that. There's a window coming up now, we'll see what happens.

“I haven't talked much, I'm just focusing on the rest of the season and we'll see.”

He has also refused to rule out remaining at Goteborg, adding: “We'll see if I stay or go. If I stay, I'll just continue with Blavitt and hopefully have a good spring too.”

Meanwhile, Goteborg's scouting manager David Vukovic will be in Lisbon this week for a TransferRoom conference – where clubs representatives and agents meet up – and has confirmed that Mansson's future will be discussed there.

“If you look at the meetings that are scheduled, there are significantly more big clubs that we will meet,” said Vukovic.

Asked if Premier League representatives will be in attendance, Vukovic added: “Yes, among other things. There are some from there, some from the Bundesliga. You can see that there is more interest now in booking meetings with IFK Goteborg than before.”

It is thought that Goteborg hold a strong negotiating position and could command a record-breaking transfer fee for his departure, with any potential deal likely to eclipse the club-record £7.6m that Brighton forked out to sign Malick Yalcouye in 2024.

Mansson is out of contract in December 2028 and his valuation will likely drop if he does not secure a move elsewhere in January or next summer.

Head Marge Liiske on Tallinn Market, One of Northern Europe’s Largest Industry-Training Confabs, Now Turning 25

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Industry @ Tallinn & Baltic Event, one of Northern Europe’s largest training and industry confabs, is celebrating its 25th anniversary with a packed program where the Baltics stand at its core, as part of Tallinn’s Black Night Film Festival’s own milestone 30th edition.

A record 900 accredited professionals from 54 countries – including around 100 sales agents, distributors and festival programmers – have signed up for the event which will showcase 71 projects (44 films and 21 series) and focus on audience design, sales and distribution through 100-plus panels, on top of industry training via the Discovery Campus strand. Related Stories Lula’s Election Win: How Fan Accounts for Taylor Swift, Billie Eilish and More Helped Him to a Historic Victory and Defeat for Trump Bill Maher Fires Back at Trump by Comparing Him to Taylor Swift

Speaking with Variety as the 12-day industry event just kickstarted, Marge Liiske, the industry boss of it all, drilled down on the program’s highlights, its past achievements and prospects for the gigantic industry showcase which has played a key role in developing the audiovisual industry in Estonia, Latvia and Lithuania.

Industry @ Tallinn & Baltic Event (ITBE) Turns 25. Could you take us through the changes that you’ve experienced since you joined the confab, and the next steps ahead?

Marge Liiske: I joined in 2013 to run the Baltic Event Co-Production Market, after seven years at the Estonian Film Foundation. In 2016 I took over the whole platform. My first priority was to merge our two strands, Industry@Tallinn and Baltic Event, so that guests would have a single entry point, program and set of services. Today that umbrella is a brand of its own, and it works seamlessly. Another boost came gradually: PÖFF [the Black Nights Film Festival] had received its A-category status in 2014, and over the following years it brought more press, sales agents and producers to Tallinn.

Since then, we have grown by adding new layers rather than by enlarging one market. Series arrived in 2017 with the world premiere of “Babylon Berlin” and have grown into TV Beats, with its conference program, co-financing market, screenings and showcases. Our talent programs grew strand by strand and are now united under the Discovery Campus, with seven programs. The short film industry program followed in 2021, children’s and youth film got its own industry days in 2023 and its own category in the Co-Production Market a year later.

Next, we want to do more for distributors and exhibitors, an area we would like to strengthen. Documentary is another: this year at least 15 documentary projects in development asked to join the co-production market, and we regularly get requests for documentary works in progress. The Discovery Campus also has exciting plans for 2027, which we will announce early next year. And we have some new ideas for the Co-Production Market, which we will reveal in spring 2027.

What are your main focal points this year?

Liiske: Each year we choose a focus. Last year it was financing; this year it is audience design, sales and distribution, both theatrical and digital. We want to give producers very hands-on tools: how to design an audience strategy from the start, when a sales agent should come on board, and how a film can reach viewers in cinemas and online. Friday Nov. 20 is dedicated entirely to sales, distribution and audience design, so producers can spend a full day with the people who will bring their films to audiences. Then our Focus Countries this year are the Baltics, with 310 Baltic professionals, 81 films, 21 projects, 19 talents in Discovery Campus. We are so excited to present them in different screenings and sessions, plus a showcase on Nov. 19. We also have a Spotlight on Basque Audiovisual with a delegation of 10 professionals, nine films and three projects.

What structural changes have you made? All 16 works in progress are on Nov. 19, and there seem to be new venues…

Liiske: We have made the schedule more concentrated. The Script Pool and Co-Production Market projects now all present on the afternoon of Wednesday Nov. 18. The works in progress follow in the morning and early afternoon of Thursday Nov. 19, leaving the teams the rest of Thursday and all of Friday to talk, mingle and build partnerships before the closing ceremony [Nov. 20]. The Discovery Campus now has its own Discovery Hub at the Tallink Spa & Conference Hotel, giving talents a home base where they can spend more time with each other.

Baltic films are thriving internationally and new studios are being built. What challenges still lie ahead for the Baltic audiovisual industry?

Liiske: The biggest challenge is turning visibility into continuity in our small markets. A “Flow,” a “Smoke Sauna Sisterhood” or a “Toxic” creates a moment, but producers need the next film, and companies need to survive between projects. Our national funds alone cannot finance ambitious work, so international partners are a necessity.

The new studios are an important step, and we present the new IDA Hub film complex at the event this year. But infrastructure also needs projects and skilled crews to fill it, and those take time to build. Keeping talent in the region while giving them international careers is part of that. And our national films need to keep finding their audiences, both at home and at festivals.

We are also very proud to showcase two Estonian startups: the IRIS casting database and Mureel platform, building sustainability and ESG compliance infrastructure for the European audiovisual industry.

What trends do you see in co-productions with the Baltics?

Liiske: Producers bring in partners earlier, and those partners come from further away: no longer only neighbours, but France, Italy, Poland and beyond. Our Co-Production Market brings together 15 projects from 10 countries this year, and eight of them are by debut directors, which shows how early international co-production now begins in a filmmaker’s career.

We also see growing interest in the Baltics from established production countries. That’s why this year we organise meetings between French and Baltic producers, and a meet-up for emerging Finnish and Estonian producers. Children’s and youth films and genre projects are other clear trends: children’s and youth projects have their own category in the market, genre projects have the European Genre Forum, and the quality keeps rising.

TV Beats continues to grow. Could you comment on this year’s program, including the screenings opening with the latest season of ‘Babylon Berlin’?

Liiske: “Babylon Berlin is a home-coming for us. The series had its world premiere at PÖFF in 2017, the year we brought series into our industry program, so it is a pleasure to present the new season here among the seven TV Beats screenings.

TV Beats has grown step by step: the forum, then the co-financing market, the screenings, and last year Coming Soon from the Baltics. This year, for the first time, we opened international calls for both the Screenings and Coming Soon.

This year’s forum looks at how creators can shape new ideas into scripts and attract international attention. It also looks both east and west, with the Finnish-Japanese journey of “Blood & Sweat” and “Dear X” from Korea. A session on the showrunner model explores how history and politics shape it across European traditions, and the European Writers Club invites writers to create stories together. And with seven series screenings, there is always something to binge between meetings.

Training of Baltic -and international- professionals-is at the core of PÖFF and ITBE. What are the Discovery Campus highlights? Could you also briefly discuss the Tallinn Industry Academy and Breaking into the Industry strands?

Liiske: Discovery Campus opens with Sir Christopher Hampton, Academy Award winner for “Dangerous Liaisons” and “The Father,” speaking about adaptation. Craft sessions follow with cinematographers Julio Macat (“Home Alone”), Roberto Schaefer (“Finding Neverland,””The Kite Runner”), David Stump (“X-Men,””Deep Blue Sea”), Dan Laustsen (“Frankenstein”) and Michael Bauman, composer Martin Phipps, production designer Inbal Weinberg (“The Room Next Door,” “Three Billboards Outside Ebbing, Missouri”), and casting directors Yngvild Haga (“Sentimental Value”), Pauline Hansson and Nina Haun (“Triangle of Sadness”), among others.

Bringing the Tallinn Industry Academy and Breaking into the Industry into the Discovery Campus this year gives emerging talents and future filmmakers more chances to meet, exchange ideas and build their networks. The Campus also has its own dedicated space for the first time, the Discovery Hub.

How are issues related to the younger generation, integrated into the Just Film Industry Days program for children and youth?

Liiske: Children and young people are tomorrow’s audience, so understanding how they think and what they value matters to the whole industry. That’s why we don’t keep children’s and youth film in a separate bubble. Just Film Industry Days, now in their fourth edition, run alongside the rest of the market, and the professionals mix.

This year, film literacy is at the heart of the programme. We look at how young viewers discover films as their access to social media changes, why audiovisual literacy deserves our attention, and what research reveals about the visual language of children’s films.

Children’s and youth projects also take part in both the Co-Production Market and the Works in Progress, so they get the same attention from sales agents and distributors as any other film.

What’s your take on current AI trends, and how is the topic explored at ITBE?

Liiske: What interests me most is not the tools themselves, but how they change who gets to tell stories, and how. AI can open doors for small teams and smaller countries like ours, but only if the human vision stays in charge. Otherwise it does not make any sense.

AI was very present in our program in recent years. We ran AI ateliers and approached AI not as something to fear, but as something we can shape. This year we have consciously taken some distance: AI has entered our lives so deeply that it is almost impossible to talk about it as a separate entity.

That doesn’t mean we’ve stopped working on it. Throughout the year, we have run Mastering AI for European Film Professionals, a five-module training program supported by Creative Europe MEDIA. In November, we keep one focused session, which asks whether AI can serve hand-crafted, cross-cultural storytelling with a human storyteller in charge. Edmond Yang, Gold winner at Project Odyssey and Gullhaien and a creative partner of Runway and Luma, will open up the workflows behind his animation and hybrid films.

How do you deal with higher costs and inflation in Estonia? Who are your main backers and key partners?

Liiske: We have had to become more efficient, but also reduce the number of free services for our guests, which of course is sad, but it’s life; Estonia has become quite an expensive country. We are also more selective about whose expenses we cover: we invest in guests who actively engage with our projects.

Our main backers are Creative Europe MEDIA, which has just renewed its support for our market, the Estonian Ministry of Culture, the Cultural Endowment of Estonia, the Estonian Film Institute, the City of Tallinn and the Estonian Business and Innovation Agency, as well as neighbouring film institutes. Our key partners include Eurimages, which gives the Co-Production Development Award, and the Council of Europe, with its €50,000 ($56,000) Series Co-Production Development Award at TV Beats. We are also very grateful to long-standing partners such as Studio Beep, ATM Virtual and many others. This year we counted 72 partners, and without exaggeration, every one of them is valuable to us.

→ Baca artikel asli

Horsham FC lose by odd goal in five to AFC Totton – the match in 38 pictures

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

The Hornets failed to take anything from their tussle with the Stags, Saturday’s game at the Fusion Aviation Community Stadium finishing 3-2.

Totton led twice in the first half only for Horsham to strike back both times through Charlie Hester-Cook and Ryan Bartley.

Josh Sims hit what proved to be the Stags’ winner on 72 minutes to bring the Hornets’ unbeaten streak to an end.

Defeat to Totton saw Horsham drop one place to 11th in the table.

The Hornets host National League South rivals Torquay United in the second round of the FA Trophy this Saturday.

Leeds United and England's Dominic Calvert-Lewin weighs in on James Trafford debate from unique vantage point

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Leeds United star Dominic Calvert-Lewin believes England are fortunate to have “two exceptional goalkeepers” in Jordan Pickford and James Trafford.

Calvert-Lewin is uniquely positioned to assess the battle for the gloves in the Three Lions squad as a former club teammate of Pickford’s and a current club teammate of Trafford’s.

Did you know with a Digital subscription to Yorkshire Post, you can get access to all of our premium content, as well as benefiting from fewer ads, loyalty rewards and much more.

He spent the best part of a decade alongside Pickford at Everton and was joined at Leeds by Trafford in the summer, when the stopper made a £40m move from Manchester City.

32-year-old Pickford has been the undisputed number one in the Three Lions fold for nearly a decade, but is being pushed hard by 24-year-old Trafford.

Calvert-Lewin said: “I think we're fortunate now that we've got two exceptional goalkeepers. One thing that is true for both of them is they're very self-confident and confident in their ability, which is what I think you need from England's number one.

“I played with Pickers for nine years at Everton, so I've seen him grow and what a career he's had for England. I don't think that can be so quickly disregarded at all.

“But now I'm playing with James Trafford, who is a younger, confident, top, top goalkeeper, and I'm seeing his quality week in, week out now. I think it's something that should be appreciated by us as a nation.

“We're very quick to kind of pit players against each other when there's all of a sudden competition for places, but instead of appreciating the fact that we've got strength in depth, and strength in depth is what you need in order to win tournaments.”

Trafford has made a strong start to life at Elland Road, impressing in what had been a problem position for Leeds in previous years.

Calvert-Lewin said: “When he (Trafford) has played, he's been top, and he's continued that coming to Leeds. I think he's been a fantastic addition for us and exactly what we needed.

“You just get that feeling when you see someone up close, and I see him on a matchday; he's got all the qualities that are needed to be a top, top goalie for me.”

Bon Appétit Pick: HelloFresh Ginger Chicken & Rice Bowls

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Over the past few years, BA editors have tested just about every meal delivery service out there, and HelloFresh keeps standing out for its bold flavors. So we teamed up with the brand on a 12-week collaboration. The BA Picks collection features recipes hand-selected by our staff, and HelloFresh customers also get an exclusive three-month digital subscription to Bon Appétit. To choose them, we cooked many, many kits in our home kitchens and in the BA Test Kitchen, following the recipe cards exactly. We judged each one on flavor, portion size, cook time, cuisine variety, and the recipe's step-by-step clarity. This Ginger Chicken and Rice Bowl is one of those picks. Here’s what it’s like to make it at home.

As a product tester, I'm very methodical, so I was excited to help build the BA Picks collection. I got to cook a bunch of meals and decide which ones were the best—the same process I take when testing the best pizza ovens or juicers. Despite being Italian, I'm constantly drawn to Asian flavors when I cook at home on weeknights. The balance of salty, sweet, spicy, and acidic is highly addictive. It hits the spot after a long day of reviewing appliances in the Test Kitchen.

That's why the Ginger Chicken & Rice Bowls caught my eye. Made with soy, Thai chili sauce, gochujang, ponzu, and Korean chili flakes, it has all the flavors I crave. It reminded me of some of my favorite BA recipes, like our Gochujang Chicken Stir-Fry, a mainstay in my weeknight rotation. Can a meal kit recipe stand up to a Bon Appétit recipe that's tested over weeks of development? It was my job to find out. (Spoiler alert: it did.)

HelloFresh's Ginger Chicken & Rice Bowls pair saucy ginger chicken with rice, a spicy cucumber side, and toasted peanuts on top. The recipe card rates it easy and lists a total time of 20 minutes. You can order it in two-, three-, four-, or six-serving sizes.

I have an entire container in my fridge dedicated to Asian condiments. If I were cooking this recipe from scratch, I'd have to lug the whole thing out, and the bottles would cover my counter. HelloFresh measures everything out for you, so all you have to do is open the bags and follow the step-by-step instructions.

This kit came with a "mini" cucumber that was more like a normal-sized one (perfect for me, since I love cucumber salad). It also had all the seasonings, rice, toppings like peanuts, and chopped chicken breast.

The pre-chopped chicken was the underrated MVP. I rarely cook chicken at home because I hate handling it raw, plus the cleanup that comes after. (Always sanitize your cutting board and prep station after working with raw meat!) So HelloFresh came in clutch here. The only ingredients I had to pull from my own pantry were oil, salt, pepper, and a small dash of sugar.

HelloFresh instructions include photos of each step, so they're easy to follow. I started by putting a pot of rice on. Then I chopped and salted the cucumbers so they could season while I cooked the chicken. After a quick sauté in a pan, I tossed the chicken in the sauce, and it was ready. Once I drained the cucumbers, all I had to do was add the chili flakes, gochujang, sesame oil, ponzu, and sugar. From start to finish, the whole process took me all of 20 minutes. That was great, because the smells coming from each component were making my mouth water.

This dish exceeded my expectations for a meal kit. The cucumbers got a ton of umami from the fermented gochujang dressing, and the chicken was saucy, with a punch of ponzu. The ginger sauce has it all: a sweet, mild ginger flavor, a trace of salty soy, and a hint of heat. Thai chili sauce and ponzu turn it into a sticky glaze for the chicken. The cucumbers add a cooling side and a pop of color, and the peanuts on top are the finishing touch. These are flavors I'd never expect in a delivery box, and I'm super impressed.

Anyone looking to explore Asian ingredients would learn a lot from this dish. It uses gochujang, ponzu, Thai chili sauce, and Korean chili flakes. I'd never cooked with Korean chili flakes (also known as gochugaru, so I was excited to taste it. If you want a quick dinner, this recipe comes together in just 20 minutes. It's a flavorful dish without project-level effort.

The Ginger Chicken & Rice Bowls hit the salty, sweet, spicy, and acidic balance I crave after a long day. The pre-chopped chicken and photo-guided steps make it easy, and there's no counter full of condiments to put away afterward. Find it in the BA Picks collection on HelloFresh, along with an exclusive three-month digital subscription to Bon Appétit.

AMD's next-gen GPU architecture has appeared in new Linux kernel patches as GFX13 updates roll out

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

The suite of GFX13 patches aren't particularly exciting in themselves, but they do suggest there might actually be some new GPUs next year.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

AMD's next-gen graphics architecture has popped up in a graphics driver for the first time, with a suite of GFX13 patches going live in an AMDGPU Linux kernel graphics driver. This has first been reported by Michael Larabel at Phoronix, who spotted a series of eight patches being posted by AMDGPU maintainer, Alex Deucher.

The RDNA 4 GPUs, the Radeon RX 9070 XT et al, were all noted under the GFX12 codename in previous patches, so it is only natural to expect that GFX13 refers to the next generation of AMD graphics cards featuring the RDNA 5 architecture.

The timing of these graphics driver patches would suggest support for the new GPUs is going into the upcoming Linux 7.5 kernel release. With Linux 7.0 dropping earlier this year, and the 7.4 version currently in release candidate testing at the moment, it is expected that the 7.5 kernel release will occur sometime in early 2027.

Wee side note for you... that's not the only interesting thing happening in Linux 7.5, however, as it will also mark the end of an era for Intel. The multimedia extensions it introduced in the Pentium era with MMX will have their last vestiges of optimisations removed in the upcoming kernel release.

At the moment, the current expectation on the actual AMD GPU release schedule itself would see one mid-range RDNA 5 card coming out in 2027, using silicon under yet another codename: AT2. That would tally neatly with the architecture's appearance in this new Linux kernel.

As for the actual code that's been dropped in this series of driver patches… it's not particularly exciting. There is a lot of code, and I am absolutely not smart nor Linux-savvy enough enough to be able to discern anything special from them, but Larabel has himself noted that as far as he can tell there isn't anything in there that reveals tasty new tidbits about the potential future RDNA 5 feature set.

But that's not to say further patches won't do that, so it will be worth keeping an eye on these updates going forward as potentially more functionality-based patches get dropped into the upcoming kernel.

The PC Gamer Circuit Breaker newsletter breaks down the latest PC gaming tech, and finds you the best deals on the best products

3. Best mid-range: Nvidia RTX 5060 Ti 8 GB

He built his first gaming PC at the tender age of 16, and finally finished bug-fixing the Cyrix-based system around a year later. When he dropped it out of the window. He first started writing for Official PlayStation Magazine and Xbox World many decades ago, then moved onto PC Format full-time, then PC Gamer, TechRadar, and T3 among others. Now he's back, writing about the nightmarish graphics card market, CPUs with more cores than sense, gaming laptops hotter than the sun, and SSDs more capacious than a Cybertruck.

Alaska Airlines Launches Global Concierge—Its Answer To United Global Services And American ConciergeKey

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Alaska Airlines has started inviting customers into Atmos Global Concierge, its new unpublished invitation-only top tier status along the lines of United Global Services, American Airlines ConciergeKey and Delta 360. The first reports point to an effort to win expensive international business class tickets, with benefits that include a next-flight guarantee during travel disruptions, dedicated assistance and Avis Chairman’s Club. Some of the early reaction has been that the benefits look underwhelming. I disagree. I used to have Avis Chairman’s Club, and it was special. And American’s next-flight guarantee came through for me when I needed it, having this benefit at Alaska could be really clutch. Invitations started surfacing November 9. One recipient shared this language: As one of our most valued status holders, you’ve been selected for two exceptional privileges: a complimentary upgrade to Atmos Titanium status and an inaugural membership in Atmos Global Concierge, our most elevated level of service. The invitation emphasizes travel across Europe, Asia and Oceania, backed by a dedicated team. Global Concierge membership begins immediately and runs through December 31, 2027. You’re invited to ATMOS GLOBAL CONCIERGE by u/iZoooom in AlaskaAirlines Alaska is giving these customers Titanium status and Global Concierge membership. What Atmos Global Concierge Members Get An invited member posted the following benefits in the discussion. Direct access to a concierge team. Proactive itinerary monitoring. A next-flight guarantee. Arrival assistance at destinations in Asia, Europe and Oceania. A car transfer at London Heathrow for tight connections. Upgrade priority on global flights. CLEAR+ membership. Avis Chairman’s Club. Another recipient reports already communicating with the team through WhatsApp. The original poster also mentioned luggage tags and being able to nominate their child for Gold status. Benefits disclosed so far lean heavily toward international service. We haven’t seen a promise of a separate boarding group, new lounge access or expanded award availability. That makes this closer, structurally, to a service overlay like Delta 360 than proof of a fully developed fifth elite tier. Who’s Getting Invited? There’s no published spending threshold. Reports suggest that expensive international business class travel matters more than a large status-point balance. The member who posted the invitation hadn’t yet reached Titanium. They expected to get there in another two weeks. They buy last-minute business class tickets to Europe and Asia, flying Alaska as well as British Airways and Japan Airlines, and hold the Atmos Summit card. They’d been trying Alaska’s Seattle–London service after years of flying British Airways. Another invitee reported more than 150,000 status points, almost all from international business class, with frequent travel to or through Heathrow and a shift toward Alaska for most flights. A Seattle-based member with more than 170,000 status points received an invitation, with roughly half the points coming from flying and promotions and half from the Summit card. Another recipient reported 261,507 status points and had already contacted the concierge team through WhatsApp. Meanwhile, a member with more than 400,000 status points, all from coach travel, reported no invitation. Neither did a Seattle-based member with 205,000 status points who said nearly all their flying was paid international business class booked two to three weeks before departure, mostly on partners but increasingly on Alaska. This fits with Alaska’s investment in premium cabins and a new Seattle business class lounge. Avis Chairman’s Club May Be The Sleeper Benefit It’s easy to skim past rental car status in an airline benefits list. We’re used to getting rental car elite cards bundled with credit cards and other memberships. Avis Chairman’s Club is nice. I used to have it. When I wrote about the program in 2007, I enjoyed upgrading a midsize reservation to the best eligible car on the lot, guaranteed availability, car delivery within 25 miles, and a valet ride back to the terminal. A manager would generally meet you at the car and help with your luggage. By 2009 I was also writing about an unpublished benefit I enjoyed: paying market price for gas. I could return the car without filling it myself and avoid the usual punitive rental car refueling price. The terminal ride was great also. You could return the car and be driven to your airline, without unloading everything to board a rental car shuttle. The Next-Flight Guarantee Could Matter Even More For an airline building a long haul network, recovery when something goes wrong matters a great deal. A missed departure can cost a full day. A dedicated phone number helps, but what matters is whether the person answering can get you onto a flight that another agent can’t book. I don’t yet have Alaska’s guarantee terms. But confirming a seat on a sold-out flight means getting where you’re going when other travelers wouldn’t. I’ve experienced what American Airlines’ ConciergeKey next-flight guarantee can do. My Austin–Miami flight was cancelled while I was on the jet bridge. Storms were disrupting Miami, and every alternative was sold out. I called the ConciergeKey desk and they confirmed me on the next nonstop to Miami, even though it was full. Alaska Airlines has grown up, with premium international service and now a service tier for its top elite customers to match. There’s no published spending threshold. Reports suggest that expensive international business class travel matters more than a large status-point balance. The member who posted the invitation hadn’t yet reached Titanium. They expected to get there in another two weeks. They buy last-minute business class tickets to Europe and Asia, flying Alaska as well as British Airways and Japan Airlines, and hold the Atmos Summit card. They’d been trying Alaska’s Seattle–London service after years of flying British Airways. Another invitee reported more than 150,000 status points, almost all from international business class, with frequent travel to or through Heathrow and a shift toward Alaska for most flights. A Seattle-based member with more than 170,000 status points received an invitation, with roughly half the points coming from flying and promotions and half from the Summit card. Another recipient reported 261,507 status points and had already contacted the concierge team through WhatsApp. Meanwhile, a member with more than 400,000 status points, all from coach travel, reported no invitation. Neither did a Seattle-based member with 205,000 status points who said nearly all their flying was paid international business class booked two to three weeks before departure, mostly on partners but increasingly on Alaska. This fits with Alaska’s investment in premium cabins and a new Seattle business class lounge. Avis Chairman’s Club May Be The Sleeper Benefit It’s easy to skim past rental car status in an airline benefits list. We’re used to getting rental car elite cards bundled with credit cards and other memberships. Avis Chairman’s Club is nice. I used to have it. When I wrote about the program in 2007, I enjoyed upgrading a midsize reservation to the best eligible car on the lot, guaranteed availability, car delivery within 25 miles, and a valet ride back to the terminal. A manager would generally meet you at the car and help with your luggage. By 2009 I was also writing about an unpublished benefit I enjoyed: paying market price for gas. I could return the car without filling it myself and avoid the usual punitive rental car refueling price. The terminal ride was great also. You could return the car and be driven to your airline, without unloading everything to board a rental car shuttle. The Next-Flight Guarantee Could Matter Even More For an airline building a long haul network, recovery when something goes wrong matters a great deal. A missed departure can cost a full day. A dedicated phone number helps, but what matters is whether the person answering can get you onto a flight that another agent can’t book. I don’t yet have Alaska’s guarantee terms. But confirming a seat on a sold-out flight means getting where you’re going when other travelers wouldn’t. I’ve experienced what American Airlines’ ConciergeKey next-flight guarantee can do. My Austin–Miami flight was cancelled while I was on the jet bridge. Storms were disrupting Miami, and every alternative was sold out. I called the ConciergeKey desk and they confirmed me on the next nonstop to Miami, even though it was full. Alaska Airlines has grown up, with premium international service and now a service tier for its top elite customers to match. Etsy Says Gah! The Handmade Marketplace and Playful Fashion Label Team Up on a Home Collection

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Since its founding in 2015, Lisa Says Gah has become known for its bowtie-pasta cardigans, checkerboard coats, and swans. Today, the California-based label is releasing its first home collection, which has a similarly playful ethos: “The Lisa Says Gah world really naturally extends into the home,” says founder Lisa Bühler. “Home and clothing ultimately tell the story of who you are.”

A pin board and two eclectic frames from Frame Mavens comprise a third of the collection.

Founder Lisa Bühler co-judged the Etsy Design Awards and selected three winners to co-design Etsy Says Gah!, her brand’s first foray into pieces for the home.

The collection is a collaboration with Etsy, a platform Bühler often uses for her own inspiration (things that make her shout: Gah!). “When you go to Etsy, you’re always going to find something that delights you,” she says.

Lisa Says Gah has stocked independent designers alongside its own line for more than a decade. This year, Bühler co-judged the Etsy Design Awards, and selected three winners to co-design Etsy Says Gah!, a nine-piece collection exclusively available via Etsy. Bühler says she prioritized “unexpected, collected things that you just don’t automatically go to when you think of homeware.”

UK-based Cait Mellor executed Bühler’s mood board through crochet in a floral table runner, a horseshoe decal, and swan coasters.

The home collection’s foundational inspiration comes from Lisa Says Gah’s two fall lines, Pastoral Daze and Swan Dance, which pair horses and swans with paisleys and cabin-inspired knits. Bühler worked with the Etsy designers by sharing mood boards, giving notes when she wanted more of a motif or a different color combination, and trusting each maker with her own medium. “They’re the expert[s],” she says. “The process felt like really trusting and putting it in their hands.”’

Each maker applied the same mood board differently. Jacqueline Sanchez, a Houston-based ceramicist who won this year’s Grand Prize for a vase covered in more than 1,500 hand-applied scales, sculpted the swan in relief on a pale pink vase wound with green vines. She also set a horse’s head in a blue baroque cameo on a cream mug and dotted a wavy platter with tiny fruit as a nod to the brand’s food prints. “[I liked] having the freedom to incorporate elements that feel really true to my work,” says Sanchez. The vase is a piece Bühler keeps coming back to: “You would just be happy to have it on the table without flowers,” she says.

The vase is the piece Bühler keeps coming back to: “You would just be happy to have it on the table without flowers,” she says.

“Because it’s our first home collection, we wanted it to feel like something you collect over time in your house versus a uniform grouping,” says Bühler.

Cait Mellor, a UK-based seller, worked the swan and horse elements into the open grid of filet crochet: swans on a set of coasters, a horseshoe under the word Lucky on a small tapestry, and flowers down a cream table runner. Her goal is to “bring this traditional art form into a more contemporary setting.” Bühler is already picturing the coasters at dinner parties, where “everyone is going to talk about the swans.”

Emma Miller and Kristen Kellar, chosen for their fabric-wrapped frames, focused on color and pattern rather than the animals. They made a burgundy frame with black polka dots on a blue background, a frame featuring blue pixelated flowers that look almost cross-stitched, and a pin board that uses pink fabric crossed with orange ribbon.

The home collection’s foundational inspiration comes from Lisa Says Gah’s two fall lines, Pastoral Daze and Swan Dance, which pair horses and swans with paisleys and cabin-inspired knits.

Bühler didn’t want the nine pieces to look like a matched set. “Because it’s our first home collection, we wanted it to feel like something you collect over time in your house versus a uniform grouping,” she says. Each item costs less than $200, and with the holidays coming—with endless-feeling opportunities for hosting and gifting—now is a great time to start collecting.

Thru-Hikers, I Am Begging You: Stop Drinking Beer Out of Your Disgusting Hiking Shoes

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Working at The Trek means that every year around this time, I get to look through a lot of thru-hiker summit photos. I always look forward to this season because of those pictures. Every one is so full of emotion: joy, relief, heartbreak, and triumph over countless unspoken obstacles.

I’ve seen hikers striking silly poses, hikers high-fiving themselves from either end of a long trail, hikers collapsed in tears before the terminus. I’ve also seen a disturbing number of photos of thru-hikers chugging beer out of their own disgusting, beat-up trail runners.

I certainly do not remember the genteel thru-hikers of my youth drinking alcoholic beverages out of their footwear, though perhaps I was a sheltered child in the world of thru-hiking and simply missed the memo. But the tradition has persisted and even seems (to me) to have become more popular in recent years, especially on the CDT.

I have attempted to rationalize this behavior in many ways. Was it simply the result of an internet trend? Were the hikers, perhaps, thinking ahead and packing out specially designated, clean shoes for this odd terminus celebration? Were they using some sort of sterile liner to keep the shoe beer free of dirt, debris, and foot flavors? I even considered that the hikers might simply be pretending to drink from beer-filled shoes for the camera.

However, after scrutinizing dozens of photos of this behavior, I have been forced to conclude that the answer to all of the above questions is no, and the answer to the original question — Why? — is, “why not?”

Only thru-hikers, I thought, could come up with such an unhinged tradition. It does seem the sort of thing we like to do: quirky, unexplainable, and gross, but in that oddly charming way only thru-hikers can pull off.

But I recently learned that this tradition is far older than America’s oldest long-distance hiking trails and that it crops up in all sorts of endurance sports. It even has a name: the “shoey.”

The best resource for learning more about shoeys is the highly informative and surprisingly detailed Wikipedia article Drinking From Shoes, which traces the tradition back at least to the Middle Ages, when drinking out of shoes was, for some reason, apparently thought to bring good luck. Based on the presumptive bacterial load of a typical thru-hiker’s well-worn shoes, I am thinking the shoey tradition could bring on a lot more than just good luck.

My dear thru-hikers, you are tough, strong, and competent, so I’m sure you know what you’re doing as you crack that can of refreshing, delicious beer and dump the contents into your sweaty, mildewy, sand-encrusted, and highly porous trail runner. So it’s probably fine, right? Right?

A shoey to celebrate the completion of the Triple Crown. Photo: Kelly “Toddler Snacks”

Blogger Kelly “Toddler Snacks” captures the magic (?) in her account of finishing the Triple Crown of hiking with a Coke shoey at the CDT southern terminus. “A shoey is a very hikertrash thing to do, and is when you pour a beverage (usually a beer) into your shoe and then chug it,” she writes. “We cheers’d shoes and put ’em treads up. I expected it to taste a lot nastier coming from a shoe that had 500 miles of foot stank, but it surprisingly just tasted like soda.”

A quick Google search reveals numerous accounts of shoey behavior right here on The Trek and on Backpacker Radio. Am I the only person who did not know about shoeys? I have never drunk beer or any other liquid out of my shoes. I must confess I do not understand the appeal. I am ashamed and can no longer face any of my colleagues.

Thru-hikers have many weird and wonderful traditions, but at least for me, this one might be the most mystifying.

So, hikers, please help me to understand the shoey tradition. Have you done one? What was it like? (Most pressingly: did it taste like feet, and did it not have bits of dirt and lint and shit floating around in it?) Did you finish your entire drink? Did you then have to put your beer- or soda-soaked shoe back onto your foot to hike out? Would you do it again?

Please tell me. I have so many questions and so few answers.

Featured image: An unsuitable shoey vessel (left). Photo via Sara. Hikers demonstrating the correct way to drink a canned beverage (right). Photo via Matthew King.

Rio Ferdinand names his top choice to replace Michael Carrick at Manchester United

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Rio Ferdinand says Mauricio Pochettino is the candidate who ‘jumps off the list’ to replace Michael Carrick at Manchester United.

Carrick, 45, looks set to be sacked following United’s disastrous start to the 2026/27 campaign.

Manchester United are 18th in the Premier League going into the November international break, just one point off bottom spot.

Old Trafford bosses are currently reviewing Carrick’s position but a change in the dugout appears to be the most likely outcome.

Eddie Howe, Fabian Hurzeler, Julian Nagelsmann and Antonio Conte have all been linked with Manchester United over the past couple of months.

Read exclusive analysis of the biggest stories in football with Metro's weekly football newsletter In The Mixer. Sign up now for free.

Pochettino has also emerged as a candidate to replace Carrick and Ferdinand has put his support behind the Argentine.

Pochettino, 54, signed an extension to his contract as USA coach after last summer’s World Cup but the deal is believed to contain a release clause.

‘People are talking about Mauricio Pochettino and it looks like he’s at the front of the queue right now,’ Ferdinand said on his YouTube channel.

‘We spent some time with him and his team in the summer, they’re really good guys and competent at what they do.

Use AI to go deeper into the stories you care about – powered by Metro and trusted publications.

‘I look at Pochettino and one of his biggest assets is that human element of his management and building confidence with his players. That’s a big part of Manchester United at the moment because we’ve got far too many broken individuals.

‘How that gets rebuilt, is that coaching, results or somebody coming in and putting an arm around their shoulder? It’s going to be all of that.

‘I look at that group of names and think Pochettino is the one that jumps off the list as someone who has more of the things we’re looking for to save and build a group of players.

‘I think he’s got more than the others from what I’ve seen and my interactions with him over time.

To view this video please enable JavaScript, and consider upgrading to a web browser that supports HTML5 video

‘There’s not many names jumping off the page when you look at it. People will say, “what has Pochettino won?”, but you can say the same about Eddie Howe.

‘The standards have dropped massively so let’s be realistic about where we are. There’s a process to get back there. We need to stabilise and get back in the Champions League again.’

Pochettino began his managerial career at Espanyol before his prominence rose during a short but impressive spell in charge of Southampton.

He then spent five years at Tottenham, taking Spurs to the final of both the Champions League and Carabao Cup but failing to win a trophy in north London.

Pochettino had mixed stints at PSG and Chelsea before taking over as coach of the United States in September 2024.

His USA team impressed in the group stages of last summer’s World Cup, topping Group D, but suffered a humbling 4-1 defeat to Belgium in the last-16.

Manchester United’s first game after the international break is a daunting trip to Anfield to face arch-rivals Liverpool on November 22.

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

What did those poor kids do to offend HBO's graphic designers?

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

In case you hadn't heard, there's a new Harry Potter series on the horizon. This December we're all hopping back on board the Hogwarts Express, only this time it's on TV, with HBO planning to serialise the entire book series over the next few years. The trailers so far have drawn sharp scrutiny – which you might expect, given the popularity of the films. And now, a new series of character posters is raising eyebrows.

What did those poor kids do to upset the editor of these posters? The designs each feature one of the principal cast members' faces in close-up, with their wand held aloft in front of it. Their faces are effectively cut in half by the wand, and some feel they look bizarrely stretched. Poor Ron seems to have been particularly afflicted. Not only that, but each half of the faces appears to be lit by a different light source, making the whole thing look weirdly spliced together. Suffice to say, these aren't among the best print ads we've seen.

New posters for HBO’s ‘HARRY POTTER’ series. Premieres December 25 on HBO. https://t.co/T8xqd9MCNDNovember 9, 2026

"Unfortunately, this is the first major mistake since the beginning of the marketing campaign. It’s already receiving a lot of negative reviews on the official pages. I’m shocked they released it like this, especially after how flawless the attention to detail has been from the very beginning," one Redditor comments. Another adds, "Each poster looks like they took one half of their face from two different photos and combined them along their wands. Absolute terrible editing work, imo."

And to add insult to injury, fans have quickly knocked together an improved take on the design, focussing on just one half of each characters' face. These are arguably more striking and dramatic, and, more importantly, the faces don't look distractingly wide.

Time will tell whether the series lives up to the books, or the original film series. But one thing's for sure: these aren't among the best posters of the year, let alone all time.

Tripwire Interactive says it's laying off 36 staff in a "necessary" decision, while reassuring that Killing Floor 3 and Maneater 2 are still in active development

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

In a statement posted yesterday, Killing Floor developers Tripwire Interactive have announced that the studio is laying off 36 "talented" internal staff as part of a "workforce reduction". The job cuts come 16 months after the release of Killing Floor 3 (which James didn't care for), and within hours of unrelated yet equally dispiriting redunancies at Skydance Games.

Tripwire's announcement claims that this was "not an easy decision, but unfortunately a necessary one for the organisation," adding that "these are truly great people who are exceptional at their roles, and we will help support these individuals with compassion and dignity during this transition." There's a small hope in me that this might mean helping those affected find new jobs quickly, though it's hard to stay optimistic when mass layoffs in games development have become such a regular occurrence.

Despite this post primarily being about the loss of 36 livelihoods, Tripwire are quick to reassure fans that Killing Floor 3's post-launch updates remain in active development, as does their comedy shark-action sequel Maneater 2. The studio "remain committed to our awesome community", they say; apparently just not to their awesome employees.

This news also arrives just days after Tripwire expanded its publishing business to support small indie developers, and less than a year after its last round of layoffs in March. Tripwire also happens to have been acquired by layoff extraordinaire corporation Embracer in 2022, just for some relevant context.

October Marks the First Month in 3 Years With a Decline in U.S. Home-Sale Prices

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

For three years, U.S. home-sale prices rose from a year earlier every single month, even as mortgage rates climbed and would-be buyers pulled back. That streak has ended.

The median home-sale price declined 0.4% year over year to $388,634 in October. That’s  the first decline since May 2023 and a sharp slowdown from the 2.5% gain just two months earlier. With Americans’ wages growing about 4% year over year, even a slight dip in prices can improve affordability. Sale prices have risen nearly every single month for the last 13 years; the only exception was a handful of months in 2023.

With the total number of homes for sale at its highest level since early 2020 and demand cooling, homebuyers’ negotiating power keeps increasing–and that’s finally showing up in sale prices.

Home-sale prices fell nationwide, but they rose in more than half of the 50 most populous U.S. metro areas. San Francisco led the nation by a wide margin, with the median sale price rising 16.8% year over year. That’s the biggest increase since 2018. Next comes New Brunswick, NJ, with a 7.7% gain. Two other NYC-area metros also had price increases ranking in the top six; the median sale price rose 4.4% in both New York City and Nassau County.

In San Francisco, AI wealth is fueling competition for scarce inventory; active listings were down 7% from a year earlier. In the New York City area, years of limited homebuilding have left buyers competing for relatively few homes in a region with a strong job market.

Home-sale prices fell most in Austin, TX, where they declined 6.5% year over year. It’s followed by Seattle (-5.1%) and Nashville, TN (-4.9%). Austin and Nashville are among the biggest buyer’s markets in the U.S., and Seattle’s shaky tech job market is curbing demand.

National sale prices are losing steam because fewer Americans can afford to buy a home. U.S. pending home sales fell 3.6% from a month earlier in October on a seasonally adjusted basis to their lowest level since 2013, with the exception of April 2020.

This is a real-time indicator of the state of the housing market; fewer homes went under contract in October largely because mortgage rates averaged 7.33%, a three-year high and up from 6.86% the month before.

All figures in this report are seasonally adjusted, with the exception of median sale price data and mortgage rate data. See a more detailed methodology here and view an interactive dashboard here.

“It’s a buyer’s market, but not many people can afford to buy,” said Chen Zhao, Redfin’s head of economics. “That could start to shift a bit, now that sale prices are closer to reflecting the shape of the market. House hunters in most of the country should know that the price they see on a listing may very well be a starting point; they should try offering below the asking price, and negotiating for closing costs, repairs and maybe even mortgage-rate buydowns. If you can’t get the price or monthly payment you want on one house, move on to the next one–with a big pool of inventory sitting on the market, buyers can afford to be picky.”

Pending home sales declined in all of the most populous U.S. metros. That’s the second month in a row that has happened; before September, the last time was three years earlier.

The biggest declines were in Seattle and San Diego, both places where pending sales fell more than 20% year over year. Those West Coast metros are some of the priciest in the U.S., so 7%-plus mortgages hit house hunters’ monthly payments there hard. In Seattle, an uncertain local job market is also curbing demand.

Closed home sales fell to their lowest level in nearly 3 years. They declined 2.1% month over month in October to their lowest level since December 2023; before that, the last time sales dropped that low was 2014. Many of the deals that closed last month went under contract in August and September, when rates were already climbing and economic uncertainty was deterring a lot of house hunters.

With homebuying demand falling, supply is building. The total number of homes for sale rose 0.9% from September to its highest level since early 2020; that’s the biggest monthly gain since May 2025.

More supply gives buyers more homes to choose from and more leverage:

“Buyers are nervous about rates, the economy, and everything they’re seeing in the news– so they need the house and the price to line up perfectly before they’ll make an offer,” said Callie Kimmel, a Redfin Premier agent in Philadelphia. “I’m advising sellers to price just below current market value from day one–and not comps from six months ago, but homes that are listed right now. Otherwise, the home is likely to end up sitting on the market for a bit and selling for less than they hoped. There are a few exceptions: Move-in ready homes in the most desirable neighborhoods are still competitive; in those cases, sellers have the upper hand.”

While total housing supply is growing, fewer sellers are adding new inventory to the pool. New listings of homes for sale fell 1% month over month to the lowest level in more than two years. Would-be sellers are pulling back as homebuying demand slows and sale prices lose steam.

The figures below are based on a list of the 50 most populous U.S. metropolitan areas. Some metros may be removed from time to time to ensure data accuracy. Refer to our metrics definition page for explanations of metrics used in this report. All changes below represent year-over-year changes.

As a data journalist at Redfin, Dana Anderson writes about the numbers behind real estate trends.
